\displaystyle(\overline{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(b)^{\dagger})(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}))\bigr)=(\overline{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}))\bigr)

as claimed.
∎

We are now in a position to show that the averaging procedure with respect to a compact quantum subgroup is compatible with multipliers.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group with a compact quantum subgroup
\mathbb{K}\subseteq\mathbb{G}
. Then the averaging map
\Xi\colon\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))

restricts to a contractive weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous map
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
.

Proof.

Recall that
\Xi(a)=\pi^{*}(h)\star a\star\pi^{*}(h)
for
a\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))
. Since
\overline{\pi^{*}(h)}=\pi^{*}(h)=\pi^{*}(h)^{\sharp}
, Lemma
5.9
tells us that
\Xi
restricts to a contractive linear map
\Xi|_{\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))}\colon\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
.
To verify weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuity it is enough to show that
(\Xi|_{\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))})^{*}
preserves
\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
. Recall the isometric homomorphism
\phi\colon\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{*}
which identifies
\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
as a closed ideal in
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{*}
. As the duality between
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
and
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
is naturally compatible
with the duality between
\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
and
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, it suffices to show that
\Xi^{*}(\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. Observe that for
b\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}),\omega\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}^{*}(\Xi^{*}(\omega))(b)=\Xi^{*}(\omega)(\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}(b))=\omega(\pi^{*}(h)\star\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}(b)\star\pi^{*}(h))\\
&=(\pi^{*}(h)\otimes\omega\otimes\pi^{*}(h))\Delta^{u,r,u}\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}(b)=(\pi^{*}(h)\star\phi(\omega)\star\pi^{*}(h))(b).\end{split}

Consequently,
\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}^{*}(\Xi^{*}(\omega))=\phi(\omega^{\prime})
for some
\omega^{\prime}\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
, and since
\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}
is surjective this shows
\Xi^{*}(\omega)=\omega^{\prime}
as required.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

Exactly the same arguments show that the one-sided averaging maps
\Xi^{l},\Xi^{r}
restrict to maps on
\A(\mathbb{G}),\B_{r}(\mathbb{G})
and
\B(\mathbb{G})
, and also to
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
.

We shall write
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{K}\backslash\mathbb{G}/\mathbb{K}))
for the image of
\Xi\colon\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
,
and refer to the elements of this space as
\mathbb{K}
-biinvariant CB multipliers of
\mathbb{G}
. Proposition
5.10
, combined with the fact that
\Xi
is a projection,
shows that
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{K}\backslash\mathbb{G}/\mathbb{K}))\subseteq\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
is a weak
∗
-closed subspace. We now study further properties of the map
\Xi
on
the level of multipliers.

Proposition 5.12
.

The averaging map
\Xi\colon\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
maps CP multipliers to CP multipliers.
Furthermore, for
a\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
and
\widehat{x}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, we have

\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})=(\pi^{*}(h)\otimes\pi^{*}(h)\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{23})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\bigr)\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}).

Proof.

Let
a\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
be arbitrary. To ease notation, write
h_{0}=\pi^{*}(h)
and
b=a\star h_{0}
.
Since
\Xi(a)=h_{0}\star(a\star h_{0})=h_{0}\star b
and
h_{0}=\overline{h_{0}}=h_{0}^{\sharp}
, formula (
5.7
) from Lemma
5.9
shows that

\displaystyle\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})

\displaystyle=(h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(b))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}))\bigr)\qquad(\widehat{x}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})).

By (
5.6
) we know that
\Theta^{l}(b)
is the map
\widehat{y}\mapsto(h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))((\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{y})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\bigr)
. Thus

\displaystyle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(b))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}))=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big((\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{23})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\big),

and hence

\displaystyle\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})

\displaystyle=(h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big((\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{23})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\big)\bigr)

\displaystyle=(h_{0}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{23})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\big).

This proves the second claim.

Assume now that
a
is a CP multiplier, i.e.
\Theta^{l}(a)\in\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
is CP. By Stinespring’s theorem and the classification of normal
*
-homomorphisms (see for
example
[
20
, Theorem I.4.4.3]
), we can find a Hilbert space
\mathscr{H}
and
r\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathscr{H}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
such
that
\Theta^{l}(a)(\widehat{x})=r^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})r
. Consequently, we can further write

\displaystyle\quad\;\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})

\displaystyle=(h_{0}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{23})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\bigr)

\displaystyle=(h_{0}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r^{*})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{14}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{24}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}\bigr)

Here we are regarding
\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
as a member of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Pick some Hilbert space
\mathscr{K}
and a universal representation
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\B(\mathscr{K})
so that
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}=\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\B(\mathscr{K})
. By choosing
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\B(\mathscr{K})
in a suitable way,
we may suppose that there is a unit vector
\xi\in\mathscr{K}
such that
\omega_{\xi}
restricted to
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime\prime}
agrees with the normal extension of
h_{0}
. Then
\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
can be regarded as a member of
\B(\mathscr{K}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, and so, for example,
\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{24}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}
is a member of
\B(\mathscr{K}\otimes\mathscr{K}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathscr{K}\otimes\mathscr{K}\otimes\mathscr{H}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
.
Thus, for
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\big(\eta_{1}\big|\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})(\eta_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\xi\otimes\xi\otimes\eta_{1}\big|\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r^{*})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{14}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{24}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{23}(\xi\otimes\xi\otimes\eta_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{14}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{13}(\xi\otimes\xi\otimes\eta_{1})\big|\xi\otimes(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\xi\otimes\eta_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\Sigma_{12}\big(\xi\otimes\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\xi\otimes\eta_{1})\big)\big|\xi\otimes(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\xi\otimes\eta_{2})\big)

where
\Sigma\in\B(\mathscr{K}\otimes\mathscr{K})
is the tensor swap map. Let
\tilde{r}\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathscr{H}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
be the operator which satisfies

\big(\alpha\big|\tilde{r}(\beta)\big)=\big(\xi\otimes\alpha\big|\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{13}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes r)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\xi\otimes\beta))\qquad(\alpha\in\mathscr{H}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\beta\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})).

Thus

\displaystyle\quad\;\big(\eta_{1}\big|\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})(\eta_{2})\big)=(\tilde{r}(\eta_{1})|(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\tilde{r}(\eta_{2})),

and so
\Theta^{l}(\Xi(a))(\widehat{x})=\tilde{r}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})\tilde{r}
is CP, as claimed.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

In general, the one-sided averaging maps
\Xi^{l},\Xi^{r}
do not map CP multipliers to CP multipliers, as we now show.

As in Remark
5.11
, the map
\Xi^{l}
restricts to
\A(\mathbb{G})
, and so induces a map
\Xi^{l}_{1}\colon\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\to\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Moreover, given
a=\widehat{\lambda}(\widehat{\omega})\in\A(\mathbb{G})
for
\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, we have
\Xi^{l}(a)=(h\pi\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta^{u,r}(a)=a\star(h\pi)=\widehat{\lambda}(\widehat{\omega}(\cdot\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})))
, as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
.
Thus
\Xi^{l}_{1}(\widehat{\omega})=\widehat{\omega}(\cdot\widehat{\pi}(p_{e}))
, and similarly
\Xi^{r}_{1}(\widehat{\omega})=\widehat{\omega}(\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})\cdot)
, for
\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
.

Suppose
\widehat{\omega}(\widehat{x}\widehat{\pi}(p_{e}))\geq 0
for all positive
\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
and all positive
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Then
\widehat{x}\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
is positive,
hence self-adjoint, so
\widehat{x}\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})=\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})\widehat{x}
for all positive
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. It follows that
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
is central.
Hence if
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is not central there exists a positive
\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\widehat{\omega}(\cdot\widehat{\pi}(p_{e}))
not positive.
Then Lemma
3.1
shows that
a=\widehat{\lambda}(\widehat{\omega})\in\A(\mathbb{G})
is a CP multiplier but
\Xi^{l}(a)=\widehat{\lambda}(\Xi^{l}_{1}(\widehat{\omega}))
is not CP.

Note that
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
is central if and only if
\Xi^{l}
agrees with the two-sided averaging map
\Xi
, compare Lemma
5.7
. Combining the above
argument with Proposition
5.12
, we therefore conclude that
\Xi^{l}
maps CP multipliers to CP multipliers if and only if
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
is central.
The same statement, with a similar proof, holds for
\Xi^{r}
.

Centrality of
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
already fails for classical locally compact groups if the compact subgroup is not normal. The case of Drinfeld doubles, explored in the next section,
gives further examples where
\widehat{\pi}(p_{e})
is not central.

6.
Drinfeld doubles of discrete quantum groups

In this section

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is a discrete quantum group. We shall use the following simplified notation:
\Delta,\widehat{\Delta}
are the coproducts
on
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
respectively,
h\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is the Haar integral,
\mathrm{W}=\mathrm{W}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}},\widehat{\mathrm{W}}=\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}

and
\ad(\mathrm{W})(x)=\mathrm{W}x\mathrm{W}^{*}
.

The Drinfeld double
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
of

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is given by
\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with the coproduct

\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\ad(\mathrm{W})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta\otimes\widehat{\Delta}),

compare
[
3
, Section 8]
. Since discrete quantum groups and their compact duals are regular, it follows from
[
3
, Proposition 9.5]
that

(6.1)

\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}).

The left and right Haar integral on
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is given by
\varphi\otimes h
, see
[
3
, Theorem 5.3, Proposition 8.1]
. The Kac–Takesaki operator of
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is

\mathrm{W}^{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}=\mathrm{W}_{13}Z_{34}^{*}\widehat{\mathrm{W}}_{24}Z_{34},

where
Z=\mathrm{W}(J\otimes\widehat{J})\mathrm{W}(J\otimes\widehat{J})
and
J,\widehat{J}
are the modular conjugations on
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}})
,
respectively, see
[
3
, Proposition 8.1]
.
The Drinfeld double
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
contains
\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}
naturally as a compact (open) quantum subgroup, with the
morphism
\pi\colon\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})

given by
\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id}
, where
\varepsilon\in\ell^{1}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is the counit. Note that since
\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}
is open, the morphism
\pi
exists at the reduced level,
which simplifies the situation compared to the previous section.

We shall consider
\mathbb{G}=D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
\mathbb{K}=\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}
. Since
\pi^{*}(h)=\varepsilon\otimes h
is a normal functional
on
\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, one easily sees that in this setting the averaging map
\Xi
defined in equation (
5.1
) is given by

(6.2)

\Xi\colon\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\ni a\mapsto(\pi^{*}(h)\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi^{*}(h))\Delta^{(2)}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(a)\in\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})),

where
\Delta^{(2)}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\colon\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\bar{\otimes}3}
is the two-fold coproduct.

Proposition 6.1
.

The image
\Xi(\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
of the averaging map
\Xi\colon\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))

equals
\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. More precisely, for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
we have

(6.3)

\Xi(x)=y\otimes\mathbbm{1}\quad\text{where}\quad y=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}((\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*}).

Similarly,
\Xi(\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
and
\Xi(\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))=\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Proof.

We use the description of
\Xi
in (
6.2
). Firstly, for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
,

(\textnormal{id}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\otimes\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(x)=(\textnormal{id}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\otimes\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})\chi_{23}\bigl(\mathrm{W}_{23}(\Delta\otimes\widehat{\Delta})(x)\mathrm{W}_{23}^{*}\bigr)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Delta})(x),

using that
\varepsilon
is a
*
-homomorphism with
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon)\Delta=\textnormal{id}
and
(\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W})=\mathbbm{1}
. It follows that

\displaystyle(\textnormal{id}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\otimes\pi^{*}(h))\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(x)

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\otimes h)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Delta})(x)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1}.

Let
z=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(x)\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and notice that

(6.4)

\displaystyle\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(z\otimes\mathbbm{1})

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{23}\Delta(z)_{12}\mathrm{W}_{23}^{*}).

Thus, using that
(\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta=\textnormal{id}
, we get

\displaystyle\Xi(x)

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon\otimes h\otimes\textnormal{id}_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})})\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(z\otimes\mathbbm{1})=(\varepsilon\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes h\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{23}\Delta(z)_{12}\mathrm{W}_{23}^{*})

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{12}(z\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}_{12}^{*})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}(z\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})\otimes\mathbbm{1},

which shows (
6.3
).

As
\mathrm{W}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and right slices of
\mathrm{W}
generate
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, it follows that an element
c\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})

is central if and only if
\mathrm{W}(c\otimes\mathbbm{1})=(c\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}
, which holds if and only if
\mathrm{W}(c\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*}=c\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. Moreover, if
c=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}(d\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})
for
some
d\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
then

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}(c\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*}

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{13}\mathrm{W}_{12}(d\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}_{12}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{13}^{*})

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h\otimes\textnormal{id})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Delta})(\mathrm{W})(d\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Delta})(\mathrm{W}^{*}))

\displaystyle=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}(d\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})\otimes\mathbbm{1}=c\otimes\mathbbm{1},

so that
c
is central. Combining this with the above formula for
\Xi(x)
implies
\Xi(\LL^{\infty}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))=\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
as claimed.

Let
x\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and set
y=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})
as in (
6.3
). We use the explicit formula for
\widehat{\mathrm{W}}=\chi(\mathrm{W}^{*})
from (
2.2
) together with (
2.1
) to see that

\displaystyle\quad\;y=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\sum_{k,l=1}^{\dim(\beta)}e^{\alpha}_{j,i}xe^{\beta}_{k,l}h\big((U^{\alpha}_{i,j})^{*}U^{\beta}_{k,l}\big)

(6.5)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}e^{\alpha}_{j,i}xe^{\alpha}_{k,j}\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{k,i}}{\Tr(\uprho_{\alpha})}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\Big(\sum_{i,k=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}x^{\alpha}_{i,k}\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{k,i}}{\Tr(\uprho_{\alpha})}\Big)p_{\alpha}.

Here we use the direct-sum matrix decomposition
x=(x^{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and
p_{\alpha}
denotes the minimal projection onto the
\alpha
block.

From this explicit formula it is clear that
\Xi
maps
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
onto
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.
Since
\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
is the norm-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
\Xi
is a conditional expectation by Lemma
5.3
,
it is also immediate that
\Xi
maps
\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
onto
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

The proof shows that the one-sided averaging map
\Xi^{r}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi^{*}(h))\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}
is given simply by
\Xi^{r}(x)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. This maps
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
onto
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, and hence
\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
onto
\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

The other one-sided averaging map
\Xi^{l}
looks more complicated, but if we start with an element of the form
x\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, then
(\pi^{*}(h)\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})=A(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1}
where

A(x)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(\mathrm{W}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})=(h\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\widehat{\mathrm{W}})\quad(x\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

We shall study this map
A
further in Section
7
.

Recall from
[
17
, Lemmas 8.4, 8.5]
that we have an isometric weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous
embedding
N\colon\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
given by
N(a)=a\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Under the embedding
N\colon\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
we have an isometric identification

\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\cong\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})),

compatible with the weak
∗
-topologies. Furthermore, these identifications preserve the property of being completely positive.

Proof.

The isometric identification
\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, compatible with the weak
∗
-topologies, is established in
[
17
, Lemma 8.6]
.

Let
a\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. According to Proposition
6.1
and Proposition
5.10
there is
b\in\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
a=\Xi(a)=b\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
.
In fact,
b\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, which can be proved following the
argument for
[
17
, Lemma 8.6]
. More precisely, it suffices to show that
b
is a cb-multiplier, and to show this, one observes that
\Theta^{l}(b\otimes\mathbbm{1})
leaves the image of
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})})
invariant and so induces a centraliser whose associated multiplier must be
b
.

Conversely, if
b\in\mathcal{Z}\M_{cb}^{l}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, then
[
17
, Lemma 8.4]
shows that

a=b\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
and Proposition
6.1
gives
\Xi(a)=a
. Thus
a\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
.

The map
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\ni a\mapsto b\in\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous because
b=(\textnormal{id}\otimes h)(a)
. It follows that the map is a weak
∗
-weak
∗
-homeomorphism, compare
[
17
, Lemma 3.7]
.

It remains to verify the claim regarding CP multipliers.
From
[
42
, Proposition 6.1]
we know that
\theta\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
is CP (i.e.
\Theta^{l}(\theta)\in\CB^{\sigma}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
is CP) if
and only if
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
is CP.
In addition, for
\theta\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
we have
\Theta^{l}(\theta)(\mathbbm{1})=\theta(e)\mathbbm{1}
, where
e\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
stands for the trivial representation.
Since
\Theta^{l}(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})
is normal, we deduce from
[
17
, Lemma 8.4]
that similarly
\Theta^{l}(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathbbm{1})=\theta(e)\mathbbm{1}
.
Now recall from
[
41
, Theorem 1.35]
that if
\mathcal{A}
is a unital
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra,
\mathscr{H}
is a Hilbert space and
u\colon\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\B(\mathscr{H})

a completely bounded linear map, then
u
is CP if and only if
\|u\|_{cb}=u(\mathbbm{1})
.
Since
\|\theta\|_{cb}=\|\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\|_{cb}
we conclude that
\theta\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
is CP if and only if
\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})))
is CP.
∎

We are now ready to state our main result. This improves in particular
[
17
, Proposition 8.9]
by removing the unimodularity condition.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be a discrete quantum group and let
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
be its Drinfeld double. Then the following conditions are equivalent.

1)

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally strongly amenable (respectively, is centrally weakly amenable, has the central Haagerup property, has central AP).

2)

D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is strongly amenable (respectively, is weakly amenable, has the Haagerup property, has AP).

Furthermore, in the weakly amenable case we have
\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

Proof.

1)\Rightarrow 2)
For AP this claim is
[
17
, Propositions 8.7, 8.9]
; let us sketch the argument. Let
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
verifying Definition
4.2
for central AP. From Proposition
6.3
(which is based on
[
42
, Proposition 6.1]
) we know that
(a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})_{i\in I}
is a net in
\M^{l}_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
converging weak
∗
to
\mathbbm{1}
. As each
a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
is in
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
it follows
that
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
has AP.

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable, the net
(a_{i})_{i\in i}
can be chosen to additionally satisfy
\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
for each
i
. By Proposition
6.3
, we have
\|a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\|_{cb}=\|a_{i}\|_{cb}
, and so
(a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})_{i\in I}
is a net bounded by
\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Invoking
[
17
, Proposition 5.7]
shows that
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weakly amenable with
\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally strongly amenable, each
a_{i}
can be chosen to be a UCP multiplier, and then
a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
will be a UCP multiplier in
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))

according to Proposition
6.3
. By Lemma
3.1
, each
a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
arises from a state, and in particular, the net
(a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})_{i\in I}
is
bounded in
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. We now invoke
[
17
, Proposition 5.6]
to conclude that
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is strongly amenable.

When

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has the central Haagerup property, the net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
can be chosen to consist of central CP multipliers forming a bounded approximate identity
for
\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. By Proposition
6.3
each
a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
is a CP multiplier of
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and
(a_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})_{i\in I}
is clearly a bounded
approximate identity for
\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))=\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

2)\Rightarrow 1)
Assume first that
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
has AP exhibited by a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}=(\lambda_{\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}}(\omega_{i}))_{i\in I}\in\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
.
Since
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})})
is in standard position on
\LL^{2}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, each
\omega_{i}
is a vector functional. By a straightforward approximation argument we may
assume that each
\omega_{i}
is of the form
\omega_{\xi_{i},\eta_{i}}
where
\xi_{i},\eta_{i}\in\Lambda_{\varphi}(\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\odot\Lambda_{h}(\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
,
here recalling that
\varphi\otimes h
is the left Haar integral on
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Then note that
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
arises from an algebraic quantum group,
compare
[
49
]
,
[
52
, Section 3.2]
, and observe that

(6.6)

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\Lambda_{\varphi\otimes h}(x),\Lambda_{\varphi\otimes h}(y)})\mathrm{W}^{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})*}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\varphi\otimes h))\bigl((\mathbbm{1}\otimes x^{*})\Delta_{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}(y)\bigr)\in\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})

for
x,y\in\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, see e.g.
[
50
, Page 12]
.
Thus each
a_{i}
is a member of
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. By Proposition
6.1
we have
\Xi(a_{i})=b_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
for some
b_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and Propositions
5.10
and
6.3
show that
b_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. Hence

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has central AP.

If
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weakly amenable we can choose a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
which is a left approximate unit in
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
in such a way that
\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. Again we may approximate each
a_{i}
by a member of
\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Since this approximation is made in the
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
norm, we may assume that
we still have
\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. Again let
\Xi(a_{i})=b_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, and observe that Propositions
5.10
and
6.3

now show
\|b_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. Given
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
denote by
p_{\alpha}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
the central projection
onto the
\alpha
component. Then
p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\odot\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
and so, as
\Xi
is a conditional expectation,

(6.7)

b_{i}p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1}=\Xi(a_{i})(p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1})=\Xi(a_{i}(p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1}))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\Xi(p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1})=p_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbbm{1},

with the convergence in
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
by Corollary
5.6
. Thus
b_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
pointwise in
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and
we conclude that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable with
\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\leq\Lambda_{cb}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
.

If
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is strongly amenable it follows from the discussion after Definition
4.1
that
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
has a bounded approximate
identity
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
where
a_{i}=\lambda_{\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}}(\omega_{i})
for some state
\omega_{i}
. By approximation, we may suppose again that

\omega_{i}=\omega_{\xi_{i}}
for
\xi_{i}\in\Lambda_{\varphi}(\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))\odot\Lambda_{h}(\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
for all
i
. Let
\Xi(a_{i})=b_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,
so that by Propositions
5.12
and
6.3
each
b_{i}
is a CP multiplier in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. As before,
b_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}

pointwise in
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, which shows that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally strongly amenable.

Finally, consider the situation where
D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
has the Haagerup property. Let
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
be a bounded approximate identity in
\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
which consists of CP multipliers. By Proposition
6.1
we obtain
