be unimodular. For
\delta>0
, the following are equivalent:

1)

\|\alpha^{*}(\widehat{x})\|\geq\delta\|\widehat{x}\|
for each
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
;

2)

\|\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x})\|\geq\delta^{2}\|\widehat{x}\|
for each
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{x}\geq 0
;

3)

\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x})\geq\delta^{2}\widehat{x}
for each
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{x}\geq 0
;

4)

\|\alpha^{*}(\widehat{x})\|\geq\delta\|\widehat{x}\|
for each
\widehat{x}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
;

5)

\|\mathfrak{Q}_{0}(\widehat{x})\|\geq\delta^{2}\|\widehat{x}\|
for each
\widehat{x}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{x}\geq 0
;

6)

\mathfrak{Q}_{0}(\widehat{x})\geq\delta^{2}\widehat{x}
for each
\widehat{x}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{x}\geq 0
;

Proof.

1)

\Leftrightarrow

2)
and
4)

\Leftrightarrow

5)

follow from (
8.9
).

2)

\Rightarrow

5)
follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{Q}
restricts to a map
\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\colon\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\to\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

2)

\Rightarrow

3)
Given
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
\epsilon>0
, notice that

(\epsilon\mathbbm{1}+\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}))^{-1/2}\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x})(\epsilon\mathbbm{1}+\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}))^{-1/2}\leq\mathbbm{1}.

As
\mathfrak{Q}
is a conditional expectation, it is a bimodule map. Set
\widehat{y}=\widehat{x}(\epsilon\mathbbm{1}+\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}))^{-1/2}
, so the previous equation shows that
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{y}^{*}\widehat{y})\leq\mathbbm{1}
. Thus
1\geq\|\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{y}^{*}\widehat{y})\|\geq\delta^{2}\|\widehat{y}^{*}\widehat{y}\|
, so
\widehat{y}^{*}\widehat{y}\leq\delta^{-2}\mathbbm{1}
. Multiplying by
(\epsilon\mathbbm{1}+\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}))^{1/2}
on both sides yields

\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}\leq\delta^{-2}(\epsilon\mathbbm{1}+\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}))\quad\text{for all }\epsilon>0,

and hence
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x})\geq\delta^{2}\widehat{x}^{*}\widehat{x}
as claimed.

5)

\Rightarrow

6)
is analogous, and the reverse implications
3)

\Rightarrow

2)
and
6)

\Rightarrow

5)
are clear.

6)
\Rightarrow
3)
follows from Kaplansky density. Indeed, given
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{x}\geq 0
, there is a bounded net of positive elements
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
a_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\widehat{x}
strongly, see
[
27
, Corollary 5.3.6]
for example. Then
(\mathfrak{Q}(a_{i})-\delta^{2}a_{i})_{i\in I}
is a net of positive elements, by
6)
, which converges weak
∗
to
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x})-\delta^{2}\widehat{x}
, showing that
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x})-\delta^{2}\widehat{x}\geq 0
, as required.
∎

Now let
G
be a compact group (always assumed to be Hausdorff) and set
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\widehat{G}
. Then
\mathfrak{Q}
is the conditional expectation of
\LL^{\infty}(G)
onto the class function algebra

\mathscr{C}_{G}=\{f\in\LL^{\infty}(G)\,|\,f(t\cdot t^{-1})=f(\cdot)\ (t\in G)\},

and similarly at the
C^{*}
-algebra level, leading to
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{G}\subseteq\mathrm{C}(G)
. It is now readily seen that
\mathfrak{Q}
takes the form

\mathfrak{Q}(f)=\int_{G}f(t\cdot t^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t\qquad(f\in\LL^{\infty}(G)),

see
[
31
, Lemma 1.2]
for example.
For
r\in G
we denote by
\operatorname{Cl}(r)=\{trt^{-1}\,|\,t\in G\}
the conjugacy class of
r
, and we write
C_{G}(r)=\{t\in G\,|\,tr=rt\}
for the centraliser of
r
in
G
. Since conjugation yields a continuous action of
G
on itself,
\operatorname{Cl}(r)
is a closed subset of
G
, and the stabiliser
C_{G}(r)
of
r\in G
is a closed subgroup of
G
.
The Orbit–Stabiliser theorem gives a bijection
G/C_{G}(r)\to\operatorname{Cl}(r);tC_{G}(r)\mapsto trt^{-1}
.

Proposition 8.11
.

Let
G
be a compact group. For
\delta>0
, the following are equivalent:

1)

\|\mathfrak{Q}(f)\|\geq\delta\|f\|
for
f\in\mathrm{C}(G)^{+}
;

2)

Each conjugacy class satisfies
|\!\operatorname{Cl}(r)|\leq\delta^{-1}
;

3)

Each centraliser
C_{G}(r)
has finite index in
G
, with
[G:C_{G}(r)]\leq\delta^{-1}
.

Proof.

By the equivalence of
5)
and
6)
in Lemma
8.10
, we see that condition
1)
is equivalent to

(8.10)

\int_{G}f(trt^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t\geq\delta f(r)\qquad(f\in\mathrm{C}(G)^{+},r\in G).

Denote by
\nu=\operatorname{d}\!t
the Haar measure on
G
, which is regular (we follow Rudin’s conventions
[
44
, Definition 2.15]
). Fix
r\in G
, and define
\theta\colon G\to G
by
\theta(t)=trt^{-1}
, a continuous map. Let
\mu\in M(G)
be the pushforward of the Haar measure,
\mu=\nu\circ\theta^{-1}
.

Then
\mu
is regular, a fact surely known, but for completeness we give a quick proof. As
\nu
is finite and
G
compact, we only need to check that
\mu
is inner regular (note
\mu(E)=1-\mu(G\setminus E)
for every Borel set
E\subseteq G
). Let
E\subseteq G
be Borel and
\epsilon>0
, so as
\nu
is regular, there is a compact
L\subseteq\theta^{-1}(E)
with
\nu(\theta^{-1}(E)\setminus L)<\epsilon
. Then
K=\theta(L)
is compact, as
\theta
is continuous, and
L\subseteq\theta^{-1}(E)\implies K\subseteq E
. Also
L\subseteq\theta^{-1}(K)
, and so
\mu(E\setminus K)=\nu(\theta^{-1}(E)\setminus\theta^{-1}(K))\leq\nu(\theta^{-1}(E)\setminus L)<\epsilon
. We conclude that
\mu
is regular as claimed.

It follows from Riesz’s theorem
[
44
, Theorem 6.19]
that
\mu
is the unique regular measure on
G
with

\int_{G}f(s)\operatorname{d}\!\mu(s)=\int_{G}f(\theta(t))\operatorname{d}\!t=\int_{G}f(trt^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t\qquad(f\in\mathrm{C}(G)).

By regularity of
\mu
we find for each
\epsilon>0
an open set
U
with
r\in U
and
\mu(U\setminus\{r\})<\epsilon
. As
U
is open and contains
r
, there is
f\colon G\to[0,1]
continuous with
f(r)=1
and
f
supported in
U
. Then

\displaystyle\nu(C_{G}(r))\leq\int_{G}f(trt^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t

\displaystyle\leq\nu\big(\{t\in G\,|\,trt^{-1}\in U\}\big)=\nu(\theta^{-1}(U))=\mu(U)

\displaystyle<\mu(\{r\})+\epsilon=\nu(\theta^{-1}(\{r\}))+\epsilon=\nu(C_{G}(r))+\epsilon.

Together with (
8.10
), this gives

\delta=\delta f(r)\leq\int_{G}f(trt^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t<\nu(C_{G}(r))+\epsilon,

and since
\epsilon>0
was arbitrary we conclude
\delta\leq\nu(C_{G}(r))
.

Let
x_{1}=e,x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\in G
be such that the cosets
(x_{j}C_{G}(r))_{j=1}^{n}
are disjoint. By invariance of the Haar measure,

1=\nu(G)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nu\big(x_{j}C_{G}(r)\big)=n\,\nu(C_{G}(r))\geq n\delta,

so
n\leq\delta^{-1}
. Thus
[G:C_{G}(r)]\leq\delta^{-1}
, and by the Orbit–Stabiliser theorem we get
|\!\operatorname{Cl}(r)|\leq\delta^{-1}
.
This argument shows that
1)

\Rightarrow

2)

\Leftrightarrow

3)
.

Conversely, assume that
2)
holds. For
r\in G
, let
(x_{i}C_{G}(r))_{i=1}^{n}
be a complete set of cosets of
C_{G}(r)
, equivalently,
\{x_{i}rx_{i}^{-1}\,|\,1\leq i\leq n\}=\operatorname{Cl}(r)
. Then for
f\in\mathrm{C}(G)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\int_{G}f(trt^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{C_{G}(r)}f(x_{i}trt^{-1}x_{i}^{-1})\operatorname{d}\!t=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu(C_{G}(r))f(x_{i}rx_{i}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\nu(C_{G}(r))\sum_{s\in\operatorname{Cl}(r)}f(s)\geq\nu(C_{G}(r))f(r).

As
1=\nu(G)=n\,\nu(C_{G}(r))
we see that
\nu(C_{G}(r))=n^{-1}\geq\delta
and so the inequality in (
8.10
) holds, thus condition
1)
holds.
∎

Recall that the derived subgroup of an abstract group
H
is the subgroup generated by all commutators
[g,h]=g^{-1}h^{-1}gh
for
g,h\in H
, denoted by
H^{\prime}
or
[H,H]
. Since
t[g,h]t^{-1}=[tgt^{-1},tht^{-1}]
for
g,h,t\in H
the subgroup
H^{\prime}
is normal. As
[g,h]\in H^{\prime}
by definition, we see that
ghH^{\prime}=hgH^{\prime}
and so
H/H^{\prime}
is abelian. Notice that
t^{-1}rt=[t,r^{-1}]r\in H^{\prime}r
, and so if
H^{\prime}
is finite, then
|\!\operatorname{Cl}(r)|\leq|H^{\prime}r|=|H^{\prime}|
, for any
r
. A remarkable theorem of Neumann shows the converse.

Theorem 8.12

(
[
39
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

Let
H
be a group such that there is a constant
n\in\mathbb{N}
with
|\!\operatorname{Cl}(r)|\leq n
for each
r\in H
. Then
H^{\prime}
is finite.

Combining this result with Proposition
8.11
, Lemma
8.10
and Proposition
8.9
we obtain a characterisation of the compact groups with
\A(D(\widehat{G})/G)=\A(\widehat{G})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Corollary 8.13
.

Let
G
be a compact group. Then
\A(D(\widehat{G})/G)=\A(\widehat{G})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
if and only if
G^{\prime}
is finite.

It follows that there are many compact groups
G
for which
\A(D(\widehat{G})/G)
is a strict subset of
\A(\widehat{G})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, for example
G=\SU(n)\,
for
n\geq 2
.
Note that when
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\Gamma
is a discrete group we have
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{r}(\Gamma)
, hence
\mathscr{C}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
\mathfrak{Q}=\textnormal{id}
. So, in this case one trivially gets
\A(D(\Gamma)/\widehat{\Gamma})=\A(\Gamma)\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Next we consider duals of free orthogonal quantum groups.

Proposition 8.14
.

Let
N\geq 2,F\in\operatorname{GL}(N,\mathbb{C})
with
\overline{F}F\in\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{1}
and
\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}=O_{F}^{+}
be the associated free orthogonal quantum group. If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is not unimodular, then
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}})\subsetneq\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. This conclusion in particular holds for
\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}=\SU_{q}(2)\,(q\in\left]-1,1\right[\setminus\{0\})
.

Proof.

The compact quantum group
O_{F}^{+}
was introduced in
[
51
]
; let us recall some of its properties, see for instance
[
47
,
4
]
. One can identify
\Irr(O_{F}^{+})
with
\mathbb{Z}_{+}
in such a way that the fundamental representation corresponds to
1
, and the fusion rules are

(8.11)

n\textnormal{$\!\!{\scriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.8pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!$}m\simeq|n-m|\oplus(|n-m|+2)\oplus\cdots\oplus(n+m)\quad(n,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}).

Furthermore every finite dimensional representation of
O_{F}^{+}
is equivalent to its contragradient. From (
8.11
) it follows that the classical and quantum dimension functions are given by
\dim(n)=[n+1]_{q_{c}}
and
\dim_{q}(n)=[n+1]_{q_{q}}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and some
0<q_{q}\leq q_{c}\leq 1
, where
[n]_{q_{x}}
are the
q
-numbers given by
[n]_{q_{x}}=\tfrac{q_{x}^{-n}-q_{x}^{n}}{q_{x}^{-1}-q_{x}}
if
0<q_{x}<1
and
[n]_{1}=n
. Since we assume that
O_{F}^{+}
is not of Kac type, we have
q_{q}<q_{c}
.

From Proposition
8.9
we know that
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, and that equality of these two vector spaces is equivalent to

\alpha^{*}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\rightarrow\{p_{z}x\,|\,x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})})\};\;\widehat{x}\mapsto p_{z}(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})

being an isomorphism. We will show that there is no
\delta>0
for which the inequalities

(8.12)

\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|\geq\delta\|\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*}\|\qquad(n\in\mathbb{N})

hold, which by (
8.9
) shows that
\alpha^{*}
is not bounded from below and proves the claim.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
and note that

(8.13)

\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})=\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n\!\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.5pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.2pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!\!n})=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{2k})=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{\dim(2k)}{\dim_{q}(2k)}\chi_{2k}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{[2k+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2k+1]_{q_{q}}}\chi_{2k}.

To calculate norm of this element in
\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}
we note that the spectrum of
\chi_{1}
in
\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}
is equal to
\left[-2,2\right]
and the restricted Haar integral is the semicircle law, see
[
47
, Corollary 6.4.12]
. Functional calculus then establishes an isomorphism
\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}\simeq\LL^{\infty}(\left[-2,2\right])
;
in the unimodular case this observation was recorded e.g. in
[
22
]
.

Choose any
0<q<1
. The quantum group
\SU_{q}(2)
can be constructed as an orthogonal quantum group for an appropriate matrix (
[
47
, Proposition 6.4.8]
), so by the above we obtain a uniquely determined isomorphism
\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}\simeq\mathscr{C}_{\SU_{q}(2)}
which maps the character
\chi_{1}
to the character
\chi_{1}^{\SU_{q}(2)}\in\LL^{\infty}(\SU_{q}(2))
. Using this isomorphism and (
8.13
) we can calculate the norm of
\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})
as follows:

(8.14)

\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|=\bigl\|\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{[2k+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2k+1]_{q_{q}}}\chi_{2k}\bigr\|_{\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}}\!=\bigl\|\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{[2k+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2k+1]_{q_{q}}}\chi_{2k}^{\SU_{q}(2)}\bigr\|_{\mathscr{C}_{\SU_{q}(2)}}\!=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{[2k+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2k+1]_{q_{q}}}(2k+1),

where in the last equality we use that
\mathrm{C}(\SU_{q}(2))
has a continuous counit. Before we bound (
8.14
), let us calculate the norm of
\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*}
in a similar way:

(8.15)

\|\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}}=\|\chi_{n}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}_{O_{F}^{+}}}=\|\chi_{n}^{\SU_{q}(2)}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}_{\SU_{q}(2)}}=(n+1)^{2}.

It is an elementary exercise that
\mathbb{N}\ni n\mapsto\frac{[2n+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2n+1]_{q_{q}}}\in\mathbb{R}
is decreasing for large enough
n
, say for
n\geq n_{0}
. Assume
n\geq n_{0}^{2}
. We have by (
8.14
)

(8.16)

\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor}(2k+1)+\frac{[2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1]_{q_{c}}}{[2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1]_{q_{q}}}\sum_{k=\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}^{n}(2k+1).

If
q_{c}=1
, we can continue as follows:

\begin{split}\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|&\leq 2(\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1)\tfrac{\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor}{2}+\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1\\
&+(2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1)\tfrac{q_{q}^{-1}-q_{q}}{q_{q}^{-2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor-1}-q_{q}^{2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}}\bigl(2(n-\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor)\tfrac{n+\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}{2}+n-\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor\bigr),\end{split}

consequently
\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|=\mathcal{O}(n)
as
n\to\infty
. If
q_{c}<1
we similarly obtain from (
8.16
)

\begin{split}\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|&\leq 2(\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1)\tfrac{\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor}{2}+\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1\\
&+\tfrac{q_{c}^{-2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor-1}-q_{c}^{2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}}{q_{c}^{-1}-q_{c}}\,\tfrac{q_{q}^{-1}-q_{q}}{q_{q}^{-2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor-1}-q_{q}^{2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}}\bigl(2(n-\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor)\tfrac{n+\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor+1}{2}+n-\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor\bigr),\end{split}

and, since the second summand is bounded by
(\tfrac{q_{c}}{q_{q}})^{-2\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor-1}
times a polynomial in
n
, we get

\|\mathfrak{Q}(\chi_{n}\chi_{n}^{*})\|=\mathcal{O}(n)
as
n\to\infty
in this case too. Together with (
8.15
) this shows that (
8.12
) cannot hold for any
\delta>0
.
∎

Another class of examples is provided by certain infinite direct sums. For notational convenience we consider only countably-infinite direct sums, but the proof below can be easily extended to the general case.

Proposition 8.15
.

Let
({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of non-classical discrete quantum groups and let
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n}
be its infinite direct sum. Then
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
8.14
, we will show that
\alpha^{*}
is not bounded from below using (
8.11
). Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n}
is not classical, there is
\beta\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n})
with
\dim(\beta)\geq 2
.
Assume first that the real part of the off-diagonal matrix coefficient
U^{\beta}_{1,\dim(\beta)}
, taken with respect to any orthonormal basis
e_{1},\dots,e_{\dim(\beta)}
, is nonzero. Define self-adjoint
\widehat{y}_{n}\in\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n})
via
\widehat{y}_{n}=t(U^{\beta}_{1,\dim(\beta)}+U^{\beta*}_{1,\dim(\beta)})
, where
t\in\mathbb{R}
is chosen such that
\|\widehat{y}_{n}\|=1\in\operatorname{Sp}(\widehat{y}_{n})
. Observe that
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{y}_{n})=0
as
1\neq\dim(\beta)
and furthermore
2\in\operatorname{Sp}(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{n})\subseteq\left[0,2\right]
. It follows that
\|\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{n}\|=2
. If the real part of
U^{\beta}_{1,\dim(\beta)}
is zero, then we construct an element
\widehat{y}_{n}
with the same properties by using the imaginary part of
U^{\beta}_{1,\dim(\beta)}
instead.

For the definition and properties of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{n}
we refer to
[
54
]
(see also
[
30
, Section 3]
). Let us only recall that the irreducible representations of
\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}
are given by

\{e^{\boxtimes\infty},\beta_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\beta_{K}\boxtimes e^{\boxtimes\infty}\,|\,K\in\mathbb{N},\beta_{1}\in\Irr(\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}_{1}),\dotsc,\beta_{K}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{K})\},

where
e^{\boxtimes\infty}
is the trivial representation on
\mathbb{C}
, while
\beta_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\beta_{K}\boxtimes e^{\boxtimes\infty}
is the representation on
\mathsf{H}_{\beta_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\beta_{K}}
with

(8.17)

U^{\beta_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\beta_{K}\boxtimes e^{\boxtimes\infty}}_{(i_{1},\dotsc,i_{K}),(j_{1},\dotsc,j_{K})}=U^{\beta_{1}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U^{\beta_{K}}_{i_{K},j_{K}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Now, choose
N\in\mathbb{N}
and consider
(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{N})\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}
in
\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Since each
\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{n}
is positive, we can write
this element as
\widehat{x}\widehat{x}^{*}
for some
\widehat{x}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Then we have

\|\widehat{x}\|^{2}=\|\widehat{x}\widehat{x}^{*}\|=\|(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{N})\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\|=\|\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{1}\|\cdots\|\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{N}\|=2^{N}.

Next observe that

\mathfrak{Q}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{K}\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty})=\mathfrak{Q}(z_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{Q}(z_{K})\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\quad(K\in\mathbb{N},z_{1}\in\Pol(\widehat{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}_{1}),\dotsc,z_{K}\in\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}_{K})),

which easily follows from (
8.17
). Using this description and
\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{y}_{n})=0
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}\widehat{x}^{*})=\mathfrak{Q}\bigl((\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(\mathbbm{1}+\widehat{y}_{N})\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{z_{1}\in\{\mathbbm{1},\widehat{y}_{1}\}}\cdots\sum_{z_{N}\in\{\mathbbm{1},\widehat{y}_{N}\}}\mathfrak{Q}(z_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{Q}(z_{N})\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}=\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}.\end{split}

We thus obtain
\|\widehat{x}\|=2^{N-1}
on the one hand, and
\|\alpha^{*}(\widehat{x})\|=\|\mathfrak{Q}(\widehat{x}\widehat{x}^{*})\|^{1/2}=1
by using (
8.9
) on the other.
Hence
\alpha^{*}
is not bounded from below.
∎

As a final remark, we consider briefly the second averaging map
\mathrm{c}_{0}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}=\mathrm{C}_{0}(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\to\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
given by
x\otimes\mathbbm{1}\mapsto A(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. As explained in Remark
7.2
, there exist non-unimodular

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

for which there are elements
x\in\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
A(x)\not\in\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. In this situation we cannot have
\A(D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, since
A(x)\otimes\mathbbm{1}\not\in\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
while
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
by Proposition
8.3
.

8.3.
Density of central algebras

Recall that the
quantum characters
are defined by

\chi_{\alpha}^{q}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\uprho_{\alpha})_{j,i}U^{\alpha}_{i,j}=\widehat{\sigma}_{-i/2}(\chi_{\alpha})\qquad(\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

Then for any
\beta\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),1\leq i,j\leq\dim(\beta)
we see that

(8.18)

h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}U^{\beta}_{i,j})=\sum_{k,l=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\uprho_{\alpha})_{k,l}h\big((U^{\alpha}_{k,l})^{*}U^{\beta}_{i,j}\big)=\frac{\delta_{\alpha,\beta}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}\sum_{k=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\uprho_{\alpha})_{k,j}(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{i,k}=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{\delta_{i,j}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}.

Thus
\widehat{\lambda}(h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot))=\dim_{q}(\alpha)^{-1}p_{\alpha}
, and so

\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\widehat{\lambda}\big(\lin\{h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\,|\,\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\}\big).

We say that
\lin\{h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\,|\,\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is the space of
finitely-supported
elements in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

Lemma 8.16
.

As
\mathfrak{Q}
is normal, it has a pre-adjoint map
\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\colon\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\to\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, which satisfies

(8.19)

\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\cdot)\big)=\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\qquad(\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),1\leq i,j\leq\dim(\alpha))

so that the space of finitely-supported element of
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is contained in the image of
\mathfrak{Q}_{*}
and also

(8.20)

\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\big)=\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\qquad(\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

Let
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
be finitely-supported, and let
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}
. Then
\omega(x)=0
.

Proof.

For
\beta\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),1\leq s,t\leq\dim(\beta)
we compute that

\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\cdot)\big)(U^{\beta}_{s,t})=h\big(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\mathfrak{Q}(U^{\beta}_{s,t})\big)=\frac{\delta_{s,t}}{\dim_{q}(\beta)}\sum_{r=1}^{\dim(\beta)}h\big(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}U^{\beta}_{r,r}\big)=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{\delta_{s,t}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)^{2}}(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i},

while

\displaystyle\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}U^{\beta}_{s,t})=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{\delta_{s,t}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)^{2}}(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i}.

by (
8.18
). As elements
U^{\beta}_{s,t}
form a basis of
\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and
\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weak
∗
-dense in
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, this establishes (
8.19
). It follows that

\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)\big)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\uprho_{\alpha})_{i,j}\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\cdot)\big)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\uprho_{\alpha})_{i,j}\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)=\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot),

which is (
8.20
).

To show that any finitely-supported element
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
annihilates
\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, it suffices to show that
h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}x)=0
for each
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}
. However, with
\omega=h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}\cdot)
, we have

h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}x)=\omega(x)=\frac{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}{\dim(\alpha)}\mathfrak{Q}_{*}(\omega)(x)=\frac{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}{\dim(\alpha)}\omega(\mathfrak{Q}(x))=0,

as required.
∎

The following is interesting in view of Lemma
7.3
above.

Proposition 8.17
.

The following are equivalent:

1)

\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is dense in
\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
for the
\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
norm;

2)

(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
.

Furthermore, the following are equivalent:

3)

\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weak
∗
-dense in
\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
;

4)

{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))=\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
.

Proof.

1)\Rightarrow 2)
By assumption, the finitely-supported elements of
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
are dense in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.
Take
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and let
(\omega_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of finitely-supported central elements converging in norm to
\omega
.
For
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, Lemma
8.16
then implies
0=\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega_{n}(x)=\omega(x)
.
We conclude
\ker\mathfrak{Q}\subseteq(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
.
Corollary
8.8
gives the reverse inclusion
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}\subseteq\ker\mathfrak{Q}
.

2)\Rightarrow 1)

Towards a contradiction, suppose that
1)
does not hold. Then, by Hahn-Banach, there is
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
which annihilates the finitely-supported elements in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, such that there is
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\omega(x)=1
. Thus
h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}x)=0
for each
\alpha
. From (
8.19
), it follows that

h(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\cdot)(\mathfrak{Q}(x))=\mathfrak{Q}_{*}\big(h(U^{\alpha*}_{i,j}\cdot)\big)(x)=\frac{(\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})_{j,i}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}h(\chi_{\alpha}^{q*}x)=0,

for each
\alpha,i,j
. As
\{h(a^{*}\cdot)\,|\,a\in\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\}
is dense in
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
it follows that
\mathfrak{Q}(x)=0
and hence
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}
.
By assumption
x\in(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
, which is a contradiction.

3)\Rightarrow 4)
By assumption, the finitely-supported elements in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
are weak
∗
-dense in
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
. Then given
\mu\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
we pick a net
(\omega_{i})_{i\in I}
of finitely-supported elements in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
converging weak
∗
to
\mu
. For
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\subseteq\ker\mathfrak{Q}
we again have
0=\lim_{i\in I}\omega_{i}(x)=\mu(x)
. Thus
{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\supseteq\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
, and together with Corollary
8.8

this shows
4)
.

4)\Rightarrow 3)
Assume that
4)
holds but
3)
does not. This means that the space of finitely-supported elements of
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is not weak
∗
dense in
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
. The weak
∗
topology of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
is given by the canonical predual
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))
, hence there is
\mu\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
and
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
which annihilates the finitely-supported elements of
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and satisfies
\mu(x)=1
. By
4)
, it follows that
x\not\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
so
\mathfrak{Q}(x)\not=0
, but the same argument as before shows that
\langle x,\mathfrak{Q}_{*}(\omega)\rangle=0
for each
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. This implies
\mathfrak{Q}(x)=0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 8.18
.

If
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
then
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is dense in
\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Furthermore, if
\B_{r}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
then
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weak
∗
-dense in
\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

Proof.

Assume that
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. By Theorem
8.4
,
\iota
and hence also
\iota^{*}
are isomorphisms. Using Corollary
8.8
we see that
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, and therefore Proposition
8.17
gives the result. The case of
the reduced Fourier-Stieltjes algebra is analogous.
∎

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is unimodular, the Haar state
h
on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is a trace we have the normal conditional expectation
F\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}
, considered already in Section
7.1
. Comparing the definition of
\mathfrak{Q}
in (
8.4
) with formula (
7.3
) shows that
\mathfrak{Q}=F
in this case.

Theorem 8.19
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be unimodular. Then
\mathfrak{Q}
has weak
∗
-closed image, and
\iota^{*}
is an isomorphism. As such,
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Proof.

The map
\mathfrak{Q}=F
is a weak
∗
-continuous idempotent and so has weak
∗
-closed image. According to Corollary
8.8
this means that
\iota^{*}
is surjective.
Moreover, Proposition
7.1
shows that
\mathfrak{Q}_{*}=F_{*}
is a projection onto
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, and hence
\ker\mathfrak{Q}=\ker F=(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
;
for this we could also use Lemma
7.3
together with Proposition
8.17
.
Due to Corollary
8.8
we conclude that
\iota^{*}
is injective. The claim now follows from Theorem
8.4
.
∎

Our next goal is to prove that density of
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
in
\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
holds under the assumption that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has central AP,
and a similar claim for the Fourier-Stieltjes algebra. As a preparation we need to establish a couple of results concerning the weak
∗
topology of
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
and central AP.

Let us first recall some facts from
[
17
, Section 4]
. If
\mathbb{G}
is a locally compact quantum group and
\mathsf{H}
a Hilbert space, then
for
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
and
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
the bounded functional

\Omega_{x,\omega}\colon\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathbb{C};\,a\mapsto\langle(\Theta^{l}(a)\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle

lies in the predual
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
. Moreover, every element of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
arises in this way, see
[
17
, Proposition 3.8]
. We shall extend this result
as follows, improving
[
23
, Proposition 1.3]
in the classical setting.

Lemma 8.20
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group and let
\mathsf{H}
be a Hilbert space.
For any
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
and
\mu\in(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H}))^{*}
, the bounded linear functional

\Omega_{x,\mu}\colon\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathbb{C};\,a\mapsto\langle\mu,(\Theta^{l}(a)\otimes\textnormal{id})x\rangle

is contained in
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
.

Proof.

By continuity and linearity, it suffices to prove the result for
x=y\otimes\theta
with
y\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\theta\in\K(\mathsf{H})
. Let
\mu^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{*}

be given by
\langle\mu^{\prime},b\rangle=\langle\mu,b\otimes\theta\rangle
for
b\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, so that

\Omega_{x,\mu}(a)=\langle\mu,\Theta^{l}(a)(y)\otimes\theta\rangle=\langle\mu^{\prime},\Theta^{l}(a)(y)\rangle.

By a further continuity argument, we may suppose that
y=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})
for some
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. As
(a\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))(\mathrm{W}^{*})
, it follows that
\Theta^{l}(a)(y)=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})((a\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{*})=(\omega a\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})
for all
a\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
. Consider
z=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\mu^{\prime})(\mathrm{W}^{*})\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, that is,
\langle z,\omega^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\mu^{\prime},(\omega^{\prime}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})\rangle
for any
\omega^{\prime}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{x,\mu}(a)

\displaystyle=\langle\mu^{\prime},(\omega a\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})\rangle=\langle z,\omega a\rangle=\langle a,z\omega\rangle.

Thus
\Omega_{x,\mu}
agrees with the action of
z\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})
, considered as an element of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
. It follows that
\Omega_{x,\mu}\in Q^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
, as required.
∎

The next result should be compared with
[
17
, Theorem 4.4]
and
[
23
, Theorem 1.9(c)]
.

Lemma 8.21
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group and let
X\subseteq\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
be a convex subset. If there is a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
X
with
a_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
, then there is a net
(b_{j})_{j\in J}
in
X
with
(\Theta^{l}(b_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x)\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}x
in norm, for each
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
and any Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
.

Proof.

By assumption and Lemma
8.20
, for any
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
and
\mu\in(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H}))^{*}
we have that

\lim_{i\in I}\langle\mu,(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x)\rangle=\lim_{i\in I}\langle a_{i},\Omega_{x,\mu}\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\mu}\rangle=\langle\mu,x\rangle.

Hence
(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x)\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}x
weakly for each
x
. Consequently, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
, the weak closure of the convex set
\bigl\{\bigl((\Theta^{l}(a)\otimes\textnormal{id})(x_{k})\bigr)_{k=1}^{n}\,\big.|\,a\in X\bigr\}
, in the
n
-fold product of
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
with itself, contains
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
. By Hahn-Banach, the same is true for the norm closure. In a standard way, we may now construct a (possibly) new net
(b_{j})_{j\in J}
in
X
so that
(\Theta^{l}(b_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x)\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}x
in norm for each
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\K(\mathsf{H})
.
∎

Proposition 8.22
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be a discrete quantum group with the central approximation property. Given
b\in\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, there is a net
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
b_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}b
weak
∗
.

Suppose further that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable. Then we can choose the net
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
to be bounded; more precisely, we can choose the net
so that
\|b_{i}\|\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\|b\|
for all
i\in I
.

Proof.

Consider the convex set
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\M_{cb}^{l}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
. By assumption, this set contains a net converging weak
∗
to
\mathbbm{1}
, and so by Lemma
8.21
there is a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x)\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}x
in norm for each
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. For every
i\in I
, there is
\widehat{\omega}_{i}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
a_{i}=\widehat{\lambda}(\widehat{\omega}_{i})
, and so
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x)=(\widehat{\omega}_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}}(x)
for
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. It follows that
\widehat{\omega}_{i}\star\mu\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mu
weak
∗
for each
\mu\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}
. As
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is an ideal in
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}
, given
b=\widehat{\lambda}(\mu)\in\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, we obtain an approximating net in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
by setting
b_{i}=\widehat{\lambda}(\widehat{\omega}_{i}\star\mu)=a_{i}b
, which converges weak
∗
to
b
.

We now consider the case when

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable. From the previous paragraph, it follows that

\widehat{\omega}_{i}\star\mu=\mu\circ\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\quad\implies\quad\|b_{i}\|_{\A(\mathbb{G})}=\|a_{i}b\|_{\A(\mathbb{G})}=\|\widehat{\omega}_{i}\star\mu\|\leq\|\mu\|\|a_{i}\|_{cb}.

Hence we can work with the convex set
\{a\in\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\,|\,\|a\|_{cb}\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\}
, and hence suppose that
\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
for each
i
. Setting
b_{i}=a_{i}b
as before then yields a net
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
with the desired properties.
∎

Theorem 8.23
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be a discrete quantum group with the central approximation property. Then
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
and
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is dense in
\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Furthermore,
{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))=\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
and
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is weak
∗
-dense in
\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

Proof.

We claim that there is a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x)\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}x
weak
∗
for each
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. This can be shown by adapting the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 4.4]
, which shows that when
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
is a net converging weak
∗
to
\mathbbm{1}
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
, and
