f\in\ell^{1}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, then the net given by
a_{i}=b_{i}\star f
will satisfy the property we need. As

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is discrete, we may take
f=\varepsilon
the counit, and then if
b_{i}
is central also

a_{i}
will be central. For each
i
let
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i})=a_{i}
, so that in particular
\omega_{i}
has finite support.

Let
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, and
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Then
\omega_{i}\star\omega
also has finite support, as
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}\star\omega)=a_{i}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. For each
i
, by (
8.20
) it follows that
\omega_{i}\star\omega=\mathfrak{Q}_{*}(\nu_{i})
for some
\nu_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
with finite support. Thus

\omega(x)=\lim_{i\in I}\langle\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x),\omega\rangle=\lim_{i\in I}\langle x\star\omega_{i},\omega\rangle=\lim_{i\in I}\langle x,\mathfrak{Q}_{*}(\nu_{i})\rangle=\lim_{i\in I}\nu_{i}(\mathfrak{Q}(x))=0.

As
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
was arbitrary it follows that
x\in(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
. We thus have
\ker\mathfrak{Q}\subseteq(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
, and
hence
\ker\mathfrak{Q}=(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
by Corollary
8.8
. Therefore Proposition
8.17
yields the first claim.

Now let
\mu\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
. Taking
X=\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
in Lemma
8.21
we can find a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
such that
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x)\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}x
in norm for all
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. We can write
a_{i}=\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i})
with
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
finitely supported. Then
\omega_{i}\star\mu
is also finitely supported in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. Consequently, for
x\in\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\subseteq\ker\mathfrak{Q}
we get
\mu(x)=0
by an analogous argument as above. We thus have
\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\subseteq{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))
, and the second claim now follows again from Corollary
8.8
and Proposition
8.17
.
∎

Remark 8.24
.

It is shown in
[
1
, Equation (3.2)]
that when

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has the central ACPAP, see
[
18
, Definition 3]
, then
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
is the closed linear span of functionals of the form
h(\cdot\chi^{q}_{\alpha})
. Recall that we can express the quantum character as
\chi_{\alpha}^{q}=\widehat{\sigma}_{-i/2}(\chi_{\alpha})
, so in particular,

\chi_{\overline{\alpha}}^{q*}=\widehat{\sigma}_{-i/2}(\chi_{\overline{\alpha}})^{*}=\widehat{\sigma}_{i/2}(\chi_{\overline{\alpha}}^{*})=\widehat{\sigma}_{i/2}(\chi_{\alpha})
. Thus for
x\in\Pol(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
we see that

h(x\chi^{q}_{\alpha})=h(x\widehat{\sigma}_{-i/2}(\chi_{\alpha}))=h(x\widehat{\sigma}_{-i}(\widehat{\sigma}_{i/2}(\chi_{\alpha})))=h(x\widehat{\sigma}_{-i}(\chi_{\overline{\alpha}}^{q*}))=h(\chi_{\overline{\alpha}}^{q*}x).

Hence
h(\cdot\chi_{\alpha}^{q})=h(\chi_{\overline{\alpha}}^{q*}\cdot)
, and so the linear span of the functionals
h(\cdot\chi_{\alpha}^{q})
agrees with the finitely-supported functionals in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, compare the discussion before Lemma
8.16
. Thus Theorem
8.23
improves this result from
[
1
]
by showing that it holds whenever

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

merely has the central AP.

With reference to Theorem
8.4
, it would be interesting to know if there is any relation between
\iota
being an isomorphism, and
\iota^{r}
being an isomorphism. In general, we do not know of any such relation, though in special cases we can say something, as follows.

Proposition 8.25
.

Suppose there exists
K>0
such that, for each
\mu\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
, there is a net
(\omega_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
converging weak
∗
to
\mu
and with
\|\omega_{i}\|\leq K\|\mu\|
for each
i\in I
. If
\iota
is an isomorphism, then also
\iota^{r}
is an isomorphism.

Note that by Proposition
8.22
the assumption in Proposition
8.25
holds whenever

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable.

Proof.

Recall that we can identify the dual space of
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))
with
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
. Consequently, the hypothesis implies that

\sup\big\{|\omega(\widehat{a})|\,|\,\omega\in\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}),\|\omega\|\leq 1\big\}\geq K^{-1}\big\|\widehat{a}+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\big\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))}

for each
\widehat{a}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. In turn, this shows that the natural map

\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\to\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{*}

is bounded below. We have the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))@>{\iota^{r}_{*}}>{}>\Xi_{0}(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}))\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}@>{\iota^{*}}>{}>\Xi_{\infty}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}))\end{CD}

As just remarked, the left-hand vertical map is bounded below, while by construction (compare Proposition
8.1
) the right-hand vertical map is isometric. It follows that if
\iota^{*}
is bounded below, then also
\iota^{r}_{*}
is bounded below. As
\iota_{*}^{r}
has norm-dense image by Proposition
8.6
, we conclude that
\iota^{r}
is an isomorphism as claimed.
∎

8.4.
Counter-examples

We now show that there are non-unimodular discrete quantum groups for which
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.
This is the case in particular for
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\widehat{\SU_{q}(2)}
, see Theorem
8.27
below.

Let us begin by estimating the norms of characters in certain quotient spaces. For more on the link between strong amenability and spectral properties of characters, in particular the Kesten amenability criterion, we refer to
[
6
, Section 3.4]
.

Lemma 8.26
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be a discrete quantum group and let
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
.

1)

The inequalities

\|\chi_{\alpha}+(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}\|_{\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}}\leq\|\chi_{\alpha}+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))}\leq\|\chi_{\alpha}\|\leq\dim(\alpha)

hold.

2)

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is strongly amenable, then
\|\chi_{\alpha}+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))}=\dim(\alpha)
.

3)

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is strongly amenable and centrally weakly amenable, then

\tfrac{\dim(\alpha)}{\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\leq\|\chi_{\alpha}+(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}\|_{\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}}.

Proof.

1)
Since
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
, we have
{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\subseteq(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
and the first point easily follows.

2)
Assume that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is strongly amenable. Then the counit defines an element
\varepsilon\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}
of norm
1
. Observe that
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
, thus for
x\in{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\subseteq\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
we have
\varepsilon(x)=0
. Consequently we can define a functional
\tilde{\varepsilon}
of norm
1
by

\tilde{\varepsilon}\colon\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\ni y+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\mapsto\varepsilon(y)\in\mathbb{C}.

Hence

\|\chi_{\alpha}+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))}\geq\bigl|\tilde{\varepsilon}\bigl(\chi_{\alpha}+{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))\bigr)\bigr|=\dim(\alpha),

and in conjunction with part
1)
this completes the proof of
2)
.

3)
Assume in addition that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable, so that there is a net
(\omega_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
in
\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
such that with
a_{\lambda}=\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{\lambda})
we have
\|\Theta^{l}(a_{\lambda})\|_{cb}\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
for each
\lambda
, and
a_{\lambda}\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{}\mathbbm{1}
pointwise. Define
\mu\in\ell^{1}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
by
\mu(e^{\beta}_{i,j})=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\delta_{i,j}
. By (
2.2
) we find
(\mu\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\mu)(\widehat{\mathrm{W}})=\chi_{\alpha}
, and so

\omega_{\lambda}(\chi_{\alpha})=(\mu\otimes\omega_{\lambda})(\mathrm{W}^{*})=\mu(a_{\lambda})\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{}\mu(\mathbbm{1})=\dim(\alpha).

As
\Theta^{l}(a_{\lambda})(x)=(\omega_{\lambda}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\Delta}(x)
for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
we see that
\omega_{\lambda}=\omega_{\lambda}\star\varepsilon=\varepsilon\circ\Theta^{l}(a_{\lambda})
as a functional on
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, so in particular
\|\omega_{\lambda}\|\leq\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. In fact, as

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is strongly amenable, it follows from
[
25
, Proposition 3.1]
that
\|\omega_{\lambda}\|=\|\Theta^{l}(a_{\lambda})\|_{cb}
. Since
\omega_{\lambda}
is central we can view it as a functional on
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{*}=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}
with norm
\|\omega_{\lambda}\|
, and so

\|\chi_{\alpha}+(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}\|_{\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}}\geq\limsup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\langle\chi_{\alpha},\tfrac{\omega_{\lambda}}{\|\omega_{\lambda}\|}\rangle\geq\tfrac{\dim(\alpha)}{\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})},

which establishes
3)
.
∎

The next theorem shows that the properties described in Theorem
8.4
do not always hold. In particular, since the dual of
\SU_{q}(2)

is strongly amenable and centrally weakly amenable they do not hold for
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\widehat{\SU_{q}(2)}
with
q\in\left]-1,1\right[\setminus\{0\}
.

Theorem 8.27
.

Let

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

be a discrete quantum group which is strongly amenable and non-unimodular.

1)

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has central AP then
\B_{r}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

2)

If

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable then
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.8
,
\iota^{r}_{*}
is a surjection if and only if
\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
has norm-closed image. If this is the case, then
\mathfrak{Q}_{0}
drops to an isomorphism
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\to\mathfrak{Q}_{0}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))
by the Open Mapping Theorem. In particular, there exists
\delta>0
with

\|\mathfrak{Q}_{0}(x)\|\geq\delta\|x+\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}}\qquad(x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

By (
8.4
), this shows that

\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}\|\chi_{\alpha}\|\geq\delta\|\chi_{\alpha}+\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}}\qquad(\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

1)
Suppose

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has central AP, so by Theorem
8.23
,
\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}={}^{\perp}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*}))
. Using Lemma
8.26
and
our assumption that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is strongly amenable, we hence conclude that

\frac{\dim(\alpha)^{2}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}=\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}\|\chi_{\alpha}\|\geq\delta\|\chi_{\alpha}+\ker\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\|=\delta\dim(\alpha)\implies\dim(\alpha)\geq\delta\dim_{q}(\alpha),

for each
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, which means that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

must be unimodular.
Indeed, given any
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
n\geq 1
, let
\alpha^{\!\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.5pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.2pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!\!n}
have decomposition
\alpha^{\!\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.5pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.2pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!\!n}=\bigoplus_{i\in I}\beta_{i}
for some finite index set
I
and
\beta_{i}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
. As the usual dimension and quantum dimension are additive and multiplicative and respect equivalence,

\dim_{q}(\alpha)^{n}=\dim_{q}(\alpha^{\!\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.5pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.2pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!\!n})=\sum_{i\in I}\dim_{q}(\beta_{i})\leq\sum_{i\in I}\delta^{-1}\dim(\beta_{i})=\delta^{-1}\dim(\alpha^{\!\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle\text{
\raisebox{0.5pt}{
\textcircled{\raisebox{-1.2pt}{$\top$}}
} }}\!\!\!n})=\delta^{-1}\dim(\alpha)^{n}.

Letting
n\rightarrow\infty
shows that
\dim_{q}(\alpha)=\dim(\alpha)
, whence

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is unimodular.
This is a contradiction to our assumptions, so
\iota^{r}_{*}
cannot be surjective, and hence cannot be an isomorphism. This means that
\iota^{r}
cannot be an isomorphism either (see for instance
[
9
, Chapter VI, Proposition 1.9]
),
and hence Theorem
8.4
yields the claim.

2)
Suppose now that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is centrally weakly amenable. We again proceed by contradiction, assuming that
\iota^{*}
is a surjection. Corollary
8.8
then implies that
\mathfrak{Q}
has weak
∗
-closed image, so
\mathfrak{Q}
has also norm-closed image (see for example
[
9
, Chapter VI, Theorem 1.10]
). Hence there exists
\delta>0
with

\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}\|\chi_{\alpha}\|\geq\delta\|\chi_{\alpha}+\ker\mathfrak{Q}\|_{\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\ker\mathfrak{Q}}\qquad(\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})).

By Theorem
8.23
we have
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, and so by Lemma
8.26
we obtain

\frac{\dim(\alpha)^{2}}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}=\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\dim_{q}(\alpha)}\|\chi_{\alpha}\|\geq\delta\frac{\dim(\alpha)}{\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\implies\dim(\alpha)\geq\frac{\delta}{\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})}\dim_{q}(\alpha).

This gives again a contradiction to our assumption that

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

is not unimodular. In the same way as above, it follows that
\iota^{*}
cannot be surjective, and hence cannot be an isomorphism. We conclude that
\iota
cannot be an isomorphism, and then Theorem
8.4
completes again the proof.
∎

Remark 8.28
.

As already indicated above, Theorem
8.27
applies in particular to
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}=\widehat{\SU_{q}(2)}
. One can obtain the conclusion
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
also in another way in this case, which we now briefly sketch. By
[
18
, Remark 31]
, there exists a bounded central functional
\omega\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
which cannot be written as a linear combination of positive functionals in
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})^{*})
.
Based on this one can show
\B_{r}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, and as
\mathcal{Z}\Lambda_{cb}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=1
by
[
18
, Theorem 24]
, Proposition
8.25
and Theorem
8.4
imply
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
.

8.5.
Summary

We conclude with a brief summary of our main results in this section. By Theorem
8.4
, we have
\A(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})=\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
if and only if
\iota
is an isomorphism. This is equivalent to
\iota^{*}
being an isomorphism, a problem which can be split into two subproblems:

•

Injectivity of
\iota^{*}
. By Corollary
8.8
this is equivalent to
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, which is further equivalent to
\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
being dense in
\mathcal{Z}\A({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
, see Proposition
8.17
. These conditions hold for unimodular

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

, Theorem
8.19
, and when

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

has the central AP, Theorem
8.23
.

•

Surjectivity of
\iota^{*}
. This is equivalent to
\iota^{*}
having weak
∗
-closed image and, by Corollary
8.8
, also equivalent to
\mathfrak{Q}
having weak
∗
-closed image. It follows from Theorem
8.27
that this property does not always hold.

The situation for
\B_{r}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}\backslash D({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})/\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
and
\mathcal{Z}\B_{r}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}
is entirely analogous. We leave open whether it is always true that
(\mathcal{Z}\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}}))^{\perp}=\ker\mathfrak{Q}
, and whether
\iota^{*}
being surjective could be equivalent to

\textstyle\mathbb{L}

being unimodular.
